}46:T12db,## 🔧 Enhanced Module Resolution for Consistent Local-First Loading

This PR addresses critical module resolution issues by enhancing the existing ModuleLoader system to provide the same local-first guarantees as server-manager.ts, ensuring consistent module loading across all CLI operations.

### 🎯 Problem Statement

The CLI was experiencing inconsistent module resolution between different components:
- **server-start.ts** was using ModuleLoader but without explicit local-first guarantees
- **server-manager.ts** had proper local-first environment setup for spawned processes
- **Inconsistent behavior** between development and production environments
- **Potential singleton pattern issues** due to mixed global/local module loading

### 🚀 Key Changes

#### 1. **Enhanced ModuleLoader System** (`packages/cli/src/utils/module-loader.ts`)
- **NEW: Environment Setup**: Added `setupEnvironment()` method that mirrors server-manager.ts logic
- **NEW: Explicit Local Detection**: Checks if local module exists before resolution
- **NEW: Enhanced Logging**: Clear feedback about which installation is being used
- **IMPROVED: Validation**: Warns if expected local module resolves to global

#### 2. **Consistent Architecture**
- **ModuleLoader** → For in-process module loading with local-first guarantees
- **server-manager.ts** → For spawned process environment setup with local-first guarantees
- **Both systems** → Use same approach: check local existence, prioritize local, set environment variables

#### 3. **Environment Variable Management**
Enhanced ModuleLoader now sets up environment like server-manager.ts:
- `NODE_PATH = "{project}/node_modules:{existing_NODE_PATH}"`
- `PATH = "{project}/node_modules/.bin:{existing_PATH}"`

### 🧪 Testing and Validation

- ✅ **Build System**: All builds pass with enhanced ModuleLoader
- ✅ **Local Module Detection**: Correctly identifies and uses local @elizaos packages
- ✅ **Environment Setup**: Properly configures NODE_PATH and PATH variables
- ✅ **Logging**: Clear feedback about local vs global module usage
- ✅ **Backward Compatibility**: All existing APIs maintained

### 📈 Impact and Benefits

#### **Development Experience**
- **Consistent Behavior**: Same module resolution logic across all CLI operations
- **Local-First Approach**: Always prioritizes project's exact dependency versions
- **Clear Feedback**: Explicit logging about which installation is being used
- **Better Debugging**: Enhanced error messages with installation guidance

#### **Architecture Improvements**
- **Unified Strategy**: Both in-process and spawned processes use same local-first approach
- **Singleton Safety**: Prevents multiple instances of core modules
- **Environment Consistency**: Standardized module resolution across all operations
- **Maintainability**: Centralized local-first logic in ModuleLoader

### 🔍 Implementation Details

#### **Enhanced Module Loading Strategy**
ModuleLoader now includes explicit local-first checks:
```typescript
const localModulePath = path.join(this.projectPath, 'node_modules', moduleName);
const isLocalModule = existsSync(localModulePath);

if (isLocalModule) {
  logger.info(`Using local ${moduleName} installation`);
} else {
  logger.info(`Using global ${moduleName} installation`);
}
```

#### **Environment Enhancement (NEW)**
Added setupEnvironment() method:
```typescript
private setupEnvironment(): void {
  // Add local node_modules to NODE_PATH
  const localModulesPath = path.join(this.projectPath, 'node_modules');
  if (existsSync(localModulesPath)) {
    process.env.NODE_PATH = `${localModulesPath}${path.delimiter}${process.env.NODE_PATH || ''}`;
  }

  // Add local .bin to PATH
  const localBinPath = path.join(this.projectPath, 'node_modules', '.bin');
  if (existsSync(localBinPath)) {
    process.env.PATH = `${localBinPath}${path.delimiter}${process.env.PATH || ''}`;
  }
}
```

### 🛠 Technical Notes

- **Enhanced, Not Replaced**: ModuleLoader was enhanced rather than replaced
- **Consistent Approach**: Both ModuleLoader and server-manager.ts use same local-first logic
- **Environment Variables**: Proper PATH and NODE_PATH setup for consistent resolution
- **Explicit Validation**: Checks that local modules are actually being used when expected
- **Comprehensive Logging**: Clear feedback about module resolution decisions

### 📦 Files Changed

- `packages/cli/src/utils/module-loader.ts` - Enhanced with local-first guarantees
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/actions/server-start.ts` - Uses enhanced ModuleLoader

This enhancement ensures that **all** @elizaos modules are resolved locally first, whether they're being loaded in the current process (ModuleLoader) or in spawned processes (server-manager.ts), providing a consistent and reliable development experience.47:T8fa,<!-- Use this template by filling in information and copying and pasting relevant items out of the HTML comments. -->

# Testing
Testing was generated through LLM, I need to review these tests in detail. I've tested the deployment to production TEE in the PR.
## Where should a reviewer start?

## Detailed testing steps
```bash
# Prerequisites:
# 1. Install the elizaos CLI: npm install -g @elizaos/cli
# 2. Ensure Docker is running and you're logged in via Docker CLI
# 3. Set TEE_MODE=PRODUCTION in your .env file

# Step 1: Login to Phala Cloud (get API key from Phala Cloud Dashboard)
elizaos tee phala auth login

# Step 2: Build Docker Image for TEE deployment 
elizaos tee phala docker build

# Step 3: Push Docker image to DockerHub
elizaos tee phala docker push

# Step 4: Create CVM (Confidential Virtual Machine) instance
elizaos tee phala cvms create -n elizaos-tee -c docker-compose.yaml --vcpu 2 --memory 4192 --disk-size 40 -e .env

# Step 5: Verify attestation (confirms TEE is running securely)
elizaos tee phala cvms attestation

# Step 6: (Optional) Upgrade CVM when you update your code
elizaos tee phala cvms upgrade -c docker-compose.yaml
```

This PR implements a comprehensive testing infrastructure for the ElizaOS CLI, addressing issue #5325. The new testing framework provides automated validation of CLI commands, documentation consistency checks, performance monitoring, and integration testing capabilities.

**Key Features:**
- Automated CLI command validation against documentation
- Performance testing for CLI responsiveness
- Error handling validation for graceful failures
- Integration with existing Bun test framework
- Documentation parsing and consistency validation

## Changes

### New Testing Infrastructure

#### Test Utilities (`tests/utils/`)
- **`cli-test-runner.ts`**: Core test runner with command execution, timeout handling, and output validation
- **`documentation-parser.ts`**: Parses and validates CLI documentation from multiple sources (Markdown, TypeScript, JSON)

#### Test Suites
- **`tests/commands/cli-validation.test.ts`**: 
  - CLI infrastructure tests (version, help, executable validation)
  - Core command validation (create, test, start, plugins)
  - Documentation consistency checks
  - Error handling tests
  - Performance benchmarks

- **`tests/integration/cli-integration.test.ts`**:
  - Project creation scenarios
  - Plugin management workflows
  - Agent runtime validation
  - Complex multi-command sequences

#### Test Setup & Execution
- **`tests/setup/test-setup.ts`**: Environment setup utilities for CI/CD integration
- **`tests/cli-test-runner.ts`**: Custom test execution framework
- **`tests/run-cli-tests.sh`**: Shell script for comprehensive test execution

### Documentation
- **`CLI-COMMANDS.md`**: Comprehensive CLI command documentation for validation

### Package Updates
- Added new test scripts to `package.json`:
  - `test:cli-validation`: Run CLI validation tests
  - `test:cli-integration`: Run integration tests
  - `test:cli-runner`: Execute custom test runner
  - `test:cli-all`: Run complete CLI test suite

## Test Results

All tests are passing with 100% success rate:

```
✅ CLI Validation Tests: 18/18 passing
✅ Core Infrastructure: Validated
✅ Documentation Consistency: Verified
✅ Error Handling: Graceful failures confirmed
✅ Performance: Sub-5 second response times
```

## Benefits

1. **Automated Validation**: Ensures CLI commands work as expected
2. **Documentation Accuracy**: Validates help text matches available commands
3. **Regression Prevention**: Catches CLI breakages before deployment
4. **Performance Monitoring**: Tracks CLI response times
5. **CI/CD Ready**: Integrates with existing testing pipelines

## Testing

To run the new CLI tests:

```bash
# Run CLI validation tests
bun run test:cli-validation

# Run integration tests
bun run test:cli-integration

# Run all CLI tests
bun run test:cli-all
```

- Updated `packages/cli/src/server/loader.ts` to import `@elizaos/plugin-grok`, ensuring the service is registered.
- Updated `packages/cli/tsconfig.json` to include path mapping for the new local plugin.
- Updated `packages/cli/src/characters/gork.ts` to demonstrate how a character can conditionally use `@elizaos/plugin-grok` if `XAI_API_KEY` is set.

This allows users with an XAI API key to use Grok models within the ElizaOS ecosystem.

4f:T83e,## Summary

This PR fixes issue #4997 where the CLI does not properly load/register dependencies from plugins. The implementation adds automatic dependency checking and installation for plugins during CLI startup and test execution.

## Changes

- Added `plugin-dependency-manager.ts` utility with functions to:
  - Check for missing plugin dependencies (regular, peer, and optional)
  - Install missing dependencies automatically
  - Validate plugin dependencies before loading
  - Perform security validation on package names and versions
  
- Integrated dependency management into:
  - `start` command: Checks and installs dependencies when loading plugins
  - `test` command: Validates dependencies before running plugin tests
  
- Added comprehensive test coverage:
  - 34 unit tests covering all functionality
  - Integration tests for end-to-end scenarios
  - 100% line coverage achieved

## Security Features

The implementation includes security validation to prevent installation of malicious packages:
- Package name validation against npm naming conventions
- Version string format validation
- Detection of suspicious package names (e.g., `sudo`, `rm`, `backdoor`)
- Refusal to install packages that fail security checks

## Technical Notes

- Removed redundant security check that was unreachable code (improved from 98.92% to 100% coverage)
- ElizaOS packages (starting with `@elizaos/`) are automatically skipped as they should already be available
- Optional dependencies are installed but failures are non-fatal
- Peer dependencies include helpful guidance for manual installation if needed

## Test Coverage

```
File                                     < /dev/null |  % Funcs | % Lines | Uncovered Line #s
----------------------------------------|---------|---------|-------------------
src/utils/plugin-dependency-manager.ts  |  100.00 |  100.00 | 
```

## Testing

1. Create a plugin with npm dependencies
2. Run `elizaos start` without pre-installing the dependencies
3. The CLI should automatically detect and install missing dependencies
4. Plugin should load successfully

57:Tb0d,# Add ELIZA_UI_ENABLE environment variable to control web UI in production

## Problem

elizaOS currently serves the web UI to anyone who can reach the server. While there's `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` for API endpoints, the web interface itself is wide open. In production deployments, this creates an unnecessary attack surface.

## Solution

Added `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE` environment variable with sensible defaults:
- **Development**: UI enabled by default
- **Production**: UI disabled by default
- **Override**: Set `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE=true/false`

When disabled, the web UI returns a standard HTTP 403 Forbidden error, but all API endpoints continue to work normally.

## Implementation

~20 lines in `packages/server/src/index.ts`:

1. Check `NODE_ENV` and `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE` to determine UI status
2. Conditionally serve static files only when UI is enabled
3. Replace SPA fallback with 403 response when UI is disabled
4. Update startup logging to clearly show UI status

## Testing

```bash
# Development (UI enabled)
bun start

# Production (UI disabled)  
NODE_ENV=production bun start

# Force enable in production
NODE_ENV=production ELIZA_UI_ENABLE=true bun start

# Force disable in development
ELIZA_UI_ENABLE=false bun start
```

When UI is disabled:
- `http://localhost:3000` → HTTP 403 Forbidden  
- `http://localhost:3000/api/server/ping` → Still works fine

## Test Coverage

Added tests:
- **Unit tests**: Environment variable parsing and default behaviours (development vs production)
- **Integration tests**: Server configuration impact and HTTP response handling
- **Security validation**: API functionality preservation when UI disabled

`bun test packages/server/src/__tests__/ui-disable-feature.test.ts`

25 tests passing, including both new tests and existing functionality verification.

## Justification

I'm working on scripting prod deploys and wanted a straighforward way to shut the UI off, but keep API open. I've purposely kept the API authentication (`ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN`) separate from this UI disable feature to minimise any clashes. 


## ⚠️ Desktop App Compatibility

Any remote deploy breaks the desktop anyway, as it's a wrapper to an iframe polling localhost:3000. 

In future iterations of the desktop app, with the correct plumbing, it could:

1. **Explicitly override**: `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE=true elizaos start`
2. **Environment file**: Add `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE=true` to `.env`
3. **Startup script**: Desktop app launcher can set the environment variable

(I'd be happy to help with this!)

## Backward compatibility

Should be fully backward compatible - no breaking changes, development experience stays the same. Only affects deployments that explicitly set `NODE_ENV=production`.

## Overview

This PR completes the migration of the Twitter plugin from unofficial GraphQL endpoints to the official Twitter API v2, removes ~3,500+ lines of legacy code, and fixes critical authentication issues that were causing 403 errors.

## Background

The Twitter plugin was using a problematic mix of:
- Unofficial GraphQL endpoints (requiring hardcoded bearer tokens)
- Legacy timeline fetching methods
- Deprecated authentication patterns
- Inconsistent error handling

This was causing:
- 403 authentication errors
- Confusion between OAuth 1.0a and OAuth 2.0
- Maintenance burden from legacy code
- Type safety issues

## Changes Made

### 1. 🔄 Complete API v2 Migration
- ✅ Migrated timeline fetching to `client.v2.homeTimeline()`
- ✅ Replaced profile fetching with `client.v2.userByUsername()` and `client.v2.user()`
- ✅ Updated followers/following to use `client.v2.following()` and `client.v2.followers()`
- ✅ Converted tweet posting to use `client.v2.tweet()`
- ✅ Removed all GraphQL query endpoints and unofficial API calls
- ✅ Eliminated `requestApi` function and hardcoded bearer tokens

### 2. 🗑️ Massive Code Cleanup
**Deleted Files (7 files, ~3,000 lines):**
- `timeline-v1.ts`
- `timeline-v2.ts` 
- `timeline-async.ts`
- `timeline-list.ts`
- `timeline-relationship.ts`
- `timeline-search.ts`
- `timeline-tweet-util.ts`
- `api.ts` (GraphQL utilities)

**Removed:**
- `rest_id` references throughout the codebase
- `parseTweet` method (no longer needed with v2 API)
- `TimelineArticle` type and `getArticle` method
- Deprecated `withCookie()` and `withXCsrfToken()` methods
- `parseTweetV2ToV1` conversion complexity

- Common error troubleshooting (#1: 403 errors)
- Clear OAuth 1.0a vs OAuth 2.0 distinction

### 5. 🛡️ Error Handling Standardization
**New file:** `utils/error-handler.ts`
- Categorized errors (AUTH, RATE_LIMIT, API, NETWORK, MEDIA, VALIDATION)
- Consistent logging patterns
- Retry logic helpers
- Fixed unsafe `error.message` access throughout

### 6. 🐛 Bug Fixes

<!-- No specific issue or ticket provided -->

# Risks

Low. This PR primarily refactors a debug script and its tests. The changes improve test reliability and portability without affecting core application logic.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR addresses two critical issues in the `scripts/debug-cli-delegation.test.ts` file:

1.  **Refactors `scripts/debug-cli-delegation.ts`**: Exports key functions from the debug script, making them directly testable.
2.  **Updates `scripts/debug-cli-delegation.test.ts`**:
    *   Modifies tests to directly import and call the exported functions, eliminating the need to spawn subprocesses.
    *   Replaces the hardcoded GitHub Actions CI path (`/home/runner/work/eliza/eliza`) with dynamic `process.cwd()` or a passed `cwd` parameter, improving local development compatibility.
    *   Adds mocking for the `@elizaos/core` logger to ensure isolated testing.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes, Improvements

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

The previous test setup for the debug script suffered from:
1.  **Ineffective Mocks**: Mocks for filesystem operations were not inherited by spawned subprocesses, leading to inconsistent test results.
2.  **Hardcoded CI Path**: The `cwd` for subprocesses was hardcoded, causing tests to fail outside of the specific CI environment.

This refactoring resolves these issues by enabling direct function testing and ensuring environment portability.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

`scripts/debug-cli-delegation.test.ts` and `scripts/debug-cli-delegation.ts`.

Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>5e:Tb94,## Summary

Implements advisory locking mechanism to prevent concurrent database migrations that could lead to corruption or errors when multiple ElizaOS instances start simultaneously in horizontally scaled environments.

Fixes #5181

## Problem

When ElizaOS is deployed in horizontally scaled environments (Kubernetes, server clusters), multiple instances starting simultaneously may attempt to run migrations at the same time, causing:
- Race conditions around table/constraint creation
- PostgreSQL errors such as "relation already exists"
- Partial or inconsistent schema state if migrations fail mid-way

## Solution

This PR introduces a robust migration locking mechanism that ensures only one instance can run migrations at a time:

### Key Features
- **PostgreSQL Advisory Locks**: Uses `pg_advisory_lock` for efficient, automatic cleanup
- **Fallback Table-Based Locking**: For databases without advisory lock support
- **Migration History Tracking**: Prevents re-running completed migrations
- **Stale Lock Cleanup**: Handles crashed processes gracefully
- **Configurable Timeout**: Default 60 seconds with automatic retry

### Implementation Details

1. **MigrationLockManager** class:
   - Acquires/releases locks using PostgreSQL advisory locks when available
   - Falls back to table-based locking for other databases
   - Tracks lock ownership with process info (PID@hostname)
   - Cleans up stale locks from crashed processes

2. **Migration History Table**:
   - Records all executed migrations with timestamps
   - Stores schema checksums to detect version changes
   - Tracks both successful and failed migrations

3. **Updated DatabaseMigrationService**:
   - Wraps all migration operations with lock acquisition
   - Ensures proper lock release even on failure
   - Works for both core system and plugin migrations

## Testing

Added comprehensive test coverage including:
- Unit tests for MigrationLockManager (100% coverage)
- Integration tests simulating concurrent migration attempts
- Edge case handling (timeouts, stale locks, failures)

## Deployment Benefits

This implementation enables safe deployment in:
- Multi-instance startup scenarios
- Auto-scaling environments
- Rolling updates
- Zero-downtime deployments

## Technical Notes

- Uses SHA256 hashing to generate deterministic lock IDs
- Non-blocking lock attempts with configurable retry logic
- Maintains compatibility with existing Drizzle migration system
- No changes required to existing plugins - works automatically

## Future Enhancements

While not required for this issue, potential future improvements could include:
- Deferred readiness in health endpoints during migrations
- Configurable `lock_timeout` and `statement_timeout` for PostgreSQL
- Exponential backoff in retry logic

---

The implementation follows PostgreSQL best practices for advisory locking and has been tested to ensure it properly serializes migration execution across multiple instances.5f:T46d,## Summary

61:T125c,# Fix: Plugin Actions Not Loading in NPM Deployed Version

## 🐛 Problem

Plugin actions were not being received by the runtime when using the NPM deployed version of the ElizaOS CLI. This issue only occurred with the published NPM package - everything worked correctly in the local monorepo development environment.

**Symptoms:**
- `elizaos create` followed by `elizaos start` with plugins would show 0 actions registered
- Simple REPLY worked but no plugin actions were available
- Bootstrap plugin reported "0 actions, 0 evaluators, 0 providers" despite having many

## 🔍 Root Cause Analysis

### 1. **Unresolved Workspace Dependencies**
The primary issue was that packages in the monorepo use `workspace:*` protocol for internal dependencies:
```json
{
  "dependencies": {
    "@elizaos/core": "workspace:*"
  }
}
```
When published to NPM, these workspace references were not resolved to actual version numbers, causing installation failures.

### 2. **Version Mismatches**
Build failures due to nx@19.8.15 requesting @nrwl packages that don't exist (they stopped at 19.8.14).

### 3. **Module Export Detection**
The plugin loader wasn't robust enough to handle different export patterns between development and production builds.

## ✅ Solution

### 1. **Dependency Resolution Script** (`prepare-packages-for-testing.sh`)
Created a script that resolves `workspace:*` to actual version numbers before packing:
```bash
# Replaces workspace:* with actual versions
sed -i.bak "s/\"workspace:\*\"/\"$VERSION\"/g" package.json
```

### 2. **Enhanced Plugin Export Detection** 
Improved the module export detection in `plugin-utils.ts` to handle multiple export patterns:
- Named export 'plugin'
- Default export
- Direct module export
- Expected function name pattern
- All exports as last resort

### 3. **Runtime Plugin Verification**
Added comprehensive logging to verify plugin registration:
```typescript
logger.info(`Registered ${registeredPlugins.length} plugins: ${registeredPlugins.join(', ')}`);
logger.info(`Registered ${registeredActions.length} actions: ${registeredActions.join(', ')}`);
```

### 4. **Version Resolution**
Fixed nx/nrwl version conflicts by adding resolutions in root `package.json`:
```json
"resolutions": {
  "nx": "19.8.14",
  "@nx/devkit": "19.8.14",
  "@nrwl/devkit": "19.8.14",
  "@nrwl/tao": "19.8.14"
}
```

### 5. **Testing Infrastructure**
Created comprehensive testing scripts to reproduce and debug NPM deployment locally:
- `test-cli-npm-pack.sh` - Tests exact NPM installation experience
- `debug-cli-plugin-loading.sh` - Debugging plugin loading issues
- `install-cli-globally.sh` - Install CLI globally from tarballs

## 📋 Changes Made

### Modified Files:
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/plugin-utils.ts` - Enhanced module export detection
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/actions/agent-start.ts` - Added plugin verification logging
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/index.ts` - Added consistent named export
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/tsup.config.ts` - Updated build configuration
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/package.json` - Added peer dependencies
- `package.json` - Added dependency resolutions

### New Files:
- `scripts/prepare-packages-for-testing.sh` - Resolves workspace dependencies
- `scripts/test-cli-npm-pack.sh` - Tests NPM deployment locally
- `scripts/debug-cli-plugin-loading.sh` - Debug plugin loading
- `scripts/install-cli-globally.sh` - Global installation script
- `CLI_TESTING_GUIDE.md` - Comprehensive testing documentation
- `PLUGIN_LOADING_SOLUTION.md` - Root cause analysis and solution

## 🧪 Testing

### To verify the fix:
```bash

./scripts/test-cli-npm-pack.sh

# Or install globally and test
./scripts/install-cli-globally.sh
elizaos create test-project
cd test-project

```

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.ai/code)63:Tde7,# Refactor: Complete Migration from JSON to XML Prompts

## Summary

This PR completes a comprehensive refactoring of all prompts across the ElizaOS codebase to use XML format instead of JSON, significantly improving LLM response reliability and consistency.

## Motivation

Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown better performance and more reliable parsing when working with XML format compared to JSON. This is particularly important for complex structured outputs where JSON parsing can be fragile due to:

- Escape character handling
- Nested object complexity
- String delimiters and special characters
- Inconsistent formatting across different LLM providers

## Changes Made

### Core Package Updates

- **entities.ts**: Updated entity resolution template to XML format for better entity matching

### Plugin-Bootstrap Actions Refactored

- **imageGeneration.ts**: Migrated image prompt generation to XML
- **reply.ts**: Updated reply template to use XML response format
- **sendMessage.ts**: Converted target extraction to XML structure
- **settings.ts**: Refactored all setting templates (success, failure, error, completion) to XML
- **updateEntity.ts**: Updated component data extraction to XML format
- **roles.ts**: Converted role assignment parsing to XML and removed `dedent` dependency
- **choice.ts**: Updated option extraction template to XML format

### Evaluators Refactored

- **reflection.ts**: Converted complex nested fact and relationship structures to XML with proper array handling

### Test Suite Updates

- **core/entities.test.ts**: Updated all entity tests to mock `parseKeyValueXml` instead of `parseJSONObjectFromText`
- **plugin-bootstrap/evaluators.test.ts**: Fixed reflection evaluator tests to handle XML responses
- **plugin-bootstrap/actions.test.ts**: Updated reply action tests to expect XML format

### Technical Improvements

1. **Consistent Parsing**: All responses now use `parseKeyValueXml` for uniform parsing
2. **Model Type Updates**: Changed from `OBJECT_LARGE/OBJECT_SMALL` to `TEXT_LARGE/TEXT_SMALL` where appropriate
3. **Better Structure Handling**: Improved handling of nested structures (arrays within XML)
4. **Type Safety**: Fixed TypeScript errors with proper type annotations
5. **Clear Instructions**: Added explicit prompting to avoid LLM reasoning sections in responses
6. **Dependency Cleanup**: Removed unnecessary `dedent` dependency from roles.ts

## XML Structure Examples

### Before (JSON):

```json
{
  "thought": "Analyzing the situation",
  "actions": ["REPLY", "UPDATE_ENTITY"],
  "text": "Hello, how can I help?"
}
```

### After (XML):

```xml
<response>
  <thought>Analyzing the situation</thought>
  <actions>REPLY,UPDATE_ENTITY</actions>
  <text>Hello, how can I help?</text>
</response>
```

## Benefits

- ✅ More reliable LLM responses across all providers
- ✅ Consistent parsing throughout the entire codebase
- ✅ Reduced parsing errors and edge cases
- ✅ Better handling of complex nested structures
- ✅ Cleaner, more maintainable code
- ✅ Improved test coverage with proper XML mocking

## Testing

All tests have been updated to handle the new XML format:

- Core entity tests now properly mock XML parsing
- Plugin-bootstrap tests updated to expect XML responses
- Test utilities enhanced to support both legacy and new formats

## Breaking Changes

None - This is an internal refactoring that maintains the same external API. All public interfaces remain unchanged.

## Changes Made

### 🔴 P0 - Critical Issues Fixed

1. **Memory Leak - Working Memory Cleanup** 
   - Implemented `MAX_WORKING_MEMORY_ENTRIES` limit of 50 entries (configurable)
   - Added automatic cleanup that removes oldest entries when limit is reached
   - Prevents unbounded memory growth during long-running action chains
   - Added debug logging for memory cleanup operations

2. **State Mutations - Immutable Updates**
   - Added helper methods `updateActionPlan()` and `updateActionStep()` for immutable updates
   - Replaced all direct mutations of `actionPlan` object with deep cloning
   - Fixed inconsistent state mutation in `updateTaskInWorkingMemory`
   - Prevents race conditions and ensures predictable state updates

3. **Type Safety - ActionResult Interface**

   - Added `createActionResult()` helper function for consistent object creation
   - Fixed all usages to ensure `success` field is always present
   - Updated all action result instantiations to use the helper

   - Ensures proper method binding and prevents runtime errors

   - Ensures CI pipeline passes with updated ActionResult interface

### 🔧 Robustness Improvements

6. **UUID Key Generation**
   - Implemented deterministic UUID generation for action plans
   - Uses SHA-256 hash of plan content for consistent keys
   - Prevents duplicate entries and ensures idempotent operations

7. **Enhanced Type Safety**
   - Added proper null checks throughout the codebase
   - Improved type assertions with runtime validation
   - Added optional chaining for safer property access

8. **Defensive Programming**
   - Added bounds checking for all array operations
   - Implemented safe property access patterns
   - Added validation for external inputs

9. **Configuration Management**
   - Made memory limits configurable via environment variables
   - Added sensible defaults with override capability
   - Improved system flexibility without code changes

   ```typescript
   // Automatic cleanup when limit exceeded
   ```

## Problem

Two minor bugs with the `elizaos create` command:

1. **Confusing directory display**: When creating a project/plugin, the confirmation prompt showed `"Create plugin 'testing' in plugin-testing?"` instead of the actual directory name (e.g., `"Create 'plugin-testing' in Desktop?"`)

2. **Abandoned directories on Ctrl-C**: Pressing Ctrl-C during `bun install` during a plugin or project installation left partially created directories behind, requiring manual cleanup

## Solution

1. **Directory display**: Added `getDisplayDirectory()` helper that gives the current directory name and simplifies absolute paths to just the directory name

2. **Cleanup on interruption**: Implemented `withCleanupOnInterrupt()` wrapper that:
   - Registers signal handlers (SIGINT/SIGTERM) before creating directories
   - Tracks directory creation state to avoid removing pre-existing directories
   - Automatically removes partially created directories on interruption
   - Shows red-colored cleanup messages for visibility

## Details

### Implementation
- Signal handlers are registered at the create function level, ensuring cleanup even when child processes (like `bun install`) receive signals directly
- Handlers are properly removed on success to prevent memory leaks
- Simplified `installDependencies` and `buildProject` functions since cleanup is now handled at a higher level

### Files Changed
- `src/commands/create/actions/creators.ts` - Added cleanup wrapper and updated all create functions
- `src/commands/create/actions/setup.ts` - Simplified after moving cleanup logic up
- `src/utils/helpers.ts` - Added `getDisplayDirectory()` helper
- `tests/commands/create.test.ts` - Added test for cleanup behavior

### Testing
Run the full test suite:
```bash
bun test tests/commands/create.test.ts
```

Manual test:
```bash
bun run dist/index.js create test-plugin --type plugin
# Press Ctrl-C during "Installing dependencies..."
# Directory should be cleaned up automatically
```

## Problem
When users install the elizaos CLI globally via `bun install -g @elizaos/cli` or npm, the server fails to find client dist files. This causes 404 errors when refreshing any route other than the home page, with error messages like:
```
[STATIC] Failed to serve index.html: Not Found
```

## Solution
Enhanced the server's path resolution logic to check multiple locations including:
- Bun's global installation path: `~/.bun/install/global/node_modules/@elizaos/cli/dist`
- npm's global installation path (via `npm root -g`)
- Common system-wide installation locations
- nvm-managed installations

## Changes
- Modified `packages/server/src/index.ts` to add comprehensive path resolution
- Added detailed logging to show all searched paths when client files aren't found
- Used the same resolved path for both static file serving and SPA fallback

## Testing
- Works correctly in monorepo development environment
- Resolves the issue for globally installed CLI packages
- Maintains backward compatibility with existing installations

74:T6bd,## Summary

This PR refactors how client distribution files are handled in the ElizaOS project. Instead of the CLI package managing the client web UI files, this responsibility is now moved to the server package.

## Changes

- **Server Package:**
  - Added `copy-client-dist.ts` script to copy client files during build
  - Updated build process to include client file copying
  - Modified server to look for client files in `dist/client` directory
  - Added `@elizaos/client` as a dependency
  - Improved client file discovery with better fallback paths

- **CLI Package:**
  - Removed `copy-client-dist.ts` script
  - Removed `copyClientDist` function from `copy-template.ts`
  - Added `@elizaos/server` to external dependencies in tsup config

- **Infrastructure:**
  - Updated package versions to 1.1.6
  - Updated dependency locks

## Motivation

This change simplifies the architecture by having the server manage its own web UI client files, rather than relying on the CLI package to host them. This makes the separation of concerns clearer and reduces coupling between packages.

## Testing

- [ ] Build client package: `cd packages/client && bun run build`
- [ ] Build server package: `cd packages/server && bun run build`
- [ ] Verify client files are copied to `packages/server/dist/client`
- [ ] Run server and verify web UI loads correctly
- [ ] Test with global installations

## Breaking Changes

This change may affect existing deployments that expect client files to be in the CLI package. Update instructions:
1. Rebuild both client and server packages
2. If using global installations, reinstall the server package
3. Update any custom deployment scripts that reference CLI dist directory for client files75:T28f2,# Overall Project Monthly Summary (July 2025)

## Executive Summary
July was a month of foundational progress for ElizaOS, focused on enhancing platform stability, developer tooling, and the user experience through a major UI overhaul. While significant strides were made in core architecture and plugin capabilities, the project also confronted critical challenges, particularly with the stability of the Twitter plugin and cross-platform compatibility on Windows.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Improving Developer Experience and Platform Stability**
*To make ElizaOS more robust, maintainable, and easier for our community to build with, we invested heavily in our foundational tooling and code quality.*
-   The command-line interface (CLI) was completely overhauled for a more intuitive and consistent user experience, and a new `plugin-quick-starter` template was added to streamline development ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
-   Developer infrastructure was strengthened with a new centralized package for test utilities, stabilized release workflows, and automated code quality analysis using AI ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
-   A major documentation overhaul was completed to create separate, clearer learning paths for both end-users and developers ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
-   The `plugin-knowledge` was made more reliable by fixing a critical data integrity bug and refactoring its configuration handling for better performance ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge)).

**Modernizing the User Interface and Core Agent Capabilities**
*To provide a more polished, professional interface and unlock more complex agent behaviors, we focused on redesigning the front end and enhancing the core framework.*
-   The web client received a significant visual redesign to align with new design standards, creating a more professional and polished interface across agent cards, chat components, and settings ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
-   A critical new **action chaining** feature was implemented, enabling agents to execute more complex, sequential tasks without human intervention ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
-   The project's core architecture was upgraded to use Bun's native `EventTarget` API, improving performance and runtime compatibility ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
-   A new multi-repository summarization feature was built, providing a more holistic view of project-wide activity ([elizaos/elizaos.github.io](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io)).

**Expanding the Plugin Ecosystem**
*To broaden the range of tools and services our AI agents can interact with, we continued to expand and modernize our plugin offerings.*
-   The official registry was expanded with several new plugins, including `plugin-desearch`, `plugin-mattermost`, and `plugin-bonsai`, increasing the platform's versatility ([elizaos-plugins/registry](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry)).
-   The Twitter plugin was modernized by completing its migration to the official Twitter API v2, removing over 3,500 lines of legacy code to improve maintainability ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter)).

**Confronting Key Integration and Compatibility Challenges**
*To ensure the framework is reliable for all users across different platforms and services, we are actively diagnosing and addressing high-impact bugs reported by the community.*
-   We are actively investigating persistent client initialization errors and API rate-limiting issues with the Twitter plugin. Community collaboration has been crucial in identifying that paid Twitter API tiers may now be required for the plugin to function reliably.
-   Several Windows-specific bugs that prevented plugins from loading were addressed, though improving cross-platform compatibility remains a key area of focus.
-   A strategic decision was made to deprecate the `plugin-local-ai` in favor of Ollama to streamline and focus our efforts on providing a better local AI development experience ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-farcaster](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-farcaster)).

### Cross-Repository Coordination

**Standardizing the Developer and User Experience**
-   A coordinated effort was made to improve consistency across the platform. This included migrating the CLI in multiple repositories to use `@clack/prompts` for a uniform command-line experience ([elizaos/eliza#5295](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/5295)) and launching a documentation overhaul to better serve both developers and end-users ([elizaos/eliza#5401](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5401)).

**Collaborative Debugging of Critical Issues**
-   Community members and maintainers collaborated across repositories like `elizaos/eliza`, `plugin-twitter`, and `registry` to diagnose persistent issues. This teamwork was essential in tracking down the root causes of the Twitter plugin's instability ([#31](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter/issues/31), [#38](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter/issues/38)) and cross-platform plugin loading failures on Windows ([#5407](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/5407)), accelerating progress toward solutions.

## Repository Spotlights

### elizaos/eliza
-   **UI/UX Overhaul:** Completed a major visual redesign of the web client, including Agent Cards ([#5351](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5351)), Chat components ([#5349](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5349)), and the main sidebar ([#5373](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5373)).
-   **CLI Enhancements:** Migrated the CLI to `@clack/prompts` for a better user experience ([#5359](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5359]) and added a `plugin-quick-starter` template for backend-only plugins ([#5589](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5589)).
-   **Core Architecture:** Implemented **action chaining** for more complex agent behaviors ([#5436](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5436)) and migrated from Node.js `EventEmitter` to Bun's `EventTarget` API for improved performance ([#5609](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5609)).
-   **Developer Tooling:** Created a new `@elizaos/test-utils` package to streamline testing ([#5507](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5507)) and introduced automated code quality analysis workflows ([#5532](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5532)).
-   **Bug Fixes:** Resolved a long-standing issue preventing plugins from loading on Windows ([#5407](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/5407)) and fixed a critical bug causing `elizaos start` to crash ([#5161](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/5161)).

### elizaos/elizaos.github.io
-   **Multi-Repository Architecture:** Implemented a new backend feature for multi-repository project summarization ([#142](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/pull/142)) and refactored the frontend to support a multi-repo UI ([#143](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/pull/143)).
-   **Code Quality:** Established a new comprehensive testing suite for queries ([#144](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/pull/144)) and removed outdated scripts and data to reduce technical debt ([#145](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/pull/145)).
-   **Active Discussions:** Engaged in critical architectural discussions around enabling agent-to-agent communication ([#5584](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/issues/5584)) and solving challenges with custom plugin schema migrations ([#5588](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/issues/5588)).

### elizaos-plugins/registry
-   **Plugin Expansion:** Expanded the ecosystem by adding `plugin-desearch` ([#187](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/187)), `plugin-mattermost` ([#188](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/188)), and `plugin-bonsai` ([#191](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/191)) to the registry.
-   **Issue Triage:** Served as a central point for diagnosing cross-cutting issues, including the Twitter plugin's instability ([#31](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/31), [#38](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/38)) and plugin loading failures on Windows ([#5407](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/5407), [#5499](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/5499)).
-   **Community Initiatives:** Fostered discussions on key improvements such as simplifying agent deployment ([#5244](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/5244)) and overhauling documentation ([#5234](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/issues/5234)).

### elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter
-   **API V2 Migration:** Completed a major refactor to finalize the migration to the official Twitter API v2, removing over 3,500 lines of legacy code ([#33](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter/pull/33)).
-   **Testing Modernization:** Began overhauling the testing framework to migrate from Vitest to Bun, aligning with project-wide standards ([#35](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter/pull/35)).
-   **Critical Stability Issues:** Focused on addressing a persistent "Client Initialization Error" that is blocking users ([#31](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter/issues/31)) and investigating API rate-limit errors ([#38](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter/issues/38)).

### elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge
-   **Code Quality:** Refactored configuration logic to improve efficiency ([#32](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/pull/32)) and enhanced type safety and logging within the `KnowledgeService` ([#33](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/pull/33)).
-   **Bug Fixes:** Resolved a critical data integrity bug that caused the same ID to be generated for every memory ([#16](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/issues/16)) and fixed inconsistent behavior in the test framework ([#5218](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/issues/5218)).
